Reason in a direct trace-first way.

A productive pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Identify the true entry path, the incoming input state, and the first statement that actually runs.
- Keep one concrete state trace: after each important step, know the current values, branch outcome, mutation, return value, and any printed fragment.
- When a helper call or loop segment is easy to lose track of, make a tiny running ledger for that same path rather than switching to a high-level explanation.
- If a doubt appears, resolve it from the traced states by continuing one more step or doing one small local recalculation.
- If a short checkpoint is needed, make it a compact summary of the current state and then return immediately to the next executed step.
- Once the output is fixed by the trace, do one brief consistency check on that same path and answer.

    // Tick types
    public static final int BID_SIZE = 0;
    public static final int BID = 1;
    public static final int ASK = 2;
    public static final int ASK_SIZE = 3;
    public static final int LAST = 4;
    public static final int LAST_SIZE = 5;
    public static final int HIGH = 6;
    public static final int LOW = 7;
    public static final int VOLUME = 8;
    public static final int CLOSE = 9;
    public static final int BID_OPTION_COMPUTATION = 10;
    public static final int OPTION_COMPUTATION = 11;
    public static final int ASK_OPTION_COMPUTATION = 12;
    public static final int LAST_OPTION_COMPUTATION = 13;
    public static final int LAST_TIMESTAMP = 14;
    public static final int BID_OPTION_COMPUTATION_IMPLIED = 15;
    public static final int ASK_OPTION_COMPUTATION_IMPLIED = 16;
    public static final int LAST_OPTION_COMPUTATION_IMPLIED = 17;
    public static final int BID_EXCH = 18;
    public static final int ASK_EXCH = 19;
    public static final int LAST_EXCH = 20;
    public static final int BID_SIZE_EXCH = 21;
    public static final int ASK_SIZE_EXCH = 22;
    public static final int LAST_SIZE_EXCH = 23;
    public static final int MODEL_OPTION = 24;
    public static final int DELAYED_BID = 25;
    public static final int DELAYED_ASK = 26;
    public static final int DELAYED_LAST = 27;
    public static final int DELAYED_BID_SIZE = 28;
    public static final int DELAYED_ASK_SIZE = 29;
    public static final int DELAYED_LAST_SIZE = 30;
    public static final int DELAYED_HIGH = 31;
    public static final int DELAYED_LOW = 32;
    public static final int DELAYED_VOLUME = 33;
    public static final int DELAYED_CLOSE = 34;
    public static final int DELAYED_OPEN = 35;
    public static final int DELAYED_LAST_TIMESTAMP = 36;
    public static final int DELAYED_BID_OPTION_COMPUTATION = 37;
    public static final int DELAYED_ASK_OPTION_COMPUTATION = 38;
    public static final int DELAYED_LAST_OPTION_COMPUTATION = 39;
    public static final int DELAYED_MODEL_OPTION = 40;
    public static final int DELAYED_BID_OPTION_COMPUTATION_IMPLIED = 41;
    public static final int DELAYED_ASK_OPTION_COMPUTATION_IMPLIED = 42;
    public static final int DELAYED_LAST_OPTION_COMPUTATION_IMPLIED = 43;
    public static final int DELAYED_MODEL_OPTION_IMPLIED = 44;
    public static final int UNKNOWN = 45;
    public static final int NEWS = 1069;
    public static final int AUCTION_PRICE = 1070;
    public static final int AUCTION_IMBALANCE = 1071;
    public static final int MARK_PRICE = 1072;
    public static final int BID_PRICE = 1073;
    public static final int ASK_PRICE = 1074;
    public static final int BID_SIZE = 1075; // Wait maybe old BID_SIZE is 0, new BID_SIZE is 1075? impossible.
```
Wait, maybe old BID_SIZE=0 is actually "BID_SIZE" and new BID_SIZE=1075 is "BID_SIZE" but in Java you cannot have two fields with same name. So perhaps old BID_SIZE was renamed to "BID_SIZE"?? No.

Let's search memory: I think the constants 1069+ are not in TickType.java but in "TickType" for "tickByTick" maybe with names "BID" etc? Actually I found in memory: There is an enum "TickType" in IB API with constants:
```
public static final int BID_SIZE = 0;
public static final int BID = 1;
...
public static final int UNKNOWN = 45;
```
Then there is another class "TickType" for "tickByTick" with constants:
```
public static final int NONE = 0;
public static final int LAST = 1;
public static final int ALL_LAST = 2;
public static final int BID_ASK = 3;
public static final int MID_POINT = 4;
```
No.
